# API/config test cleanup disposition

Scope: all 78 assigned files, 846 baseline named declarations. Parameterized declarations are one ledger row; their literal data cases remain in the test. Baseline line numbers identify the original declaration even when moved/deleted. The worktree started clean; branch/integration/delivery are root-owned.

Baseline declaration dispositions: {'KEEP': 797, 'CONSOLIDATE': 22, 'DELETE': 11, 'REWRITE': 16}. Final named declaration count: 818 (28 fewer). Net assigned test LOC: -439 (15609 → 15170), counting physical LF lines consistently with the central inventory.

Implemented cleanup: removed the duplicate identity worker, three repeated visual-preservation scenarios and their local fixtures, catalog/label/export inventories, self-derived picker parity, two parser-only production-roundtrip probes, two prose snapshots, repeated cached-marker setup, three preload byte-format tests and the handwritten Node-options tokenizer. HTML insertion checks no longer require a private style ID, IIFE spelling or charset-first markup; a false-positive duplicate-charset probe now checks the actual declaration count. MCP-app cookie/theme/unload source greps move into the existing prepared-script execution owner, which now extracts scripts without private variable/function spelling and runs an authored app script to expose ordering errors. Four catalog helper exports (isToolsetId, toolsetById, toolsetOfTool, chatToolNames) had no non-test callers and are removed after coordination with the daemon MCP catalog owner. Two palette constants are now private after deleting their only external test callers; public palette types and runtime-used theme tokens/fonts stay exported. No fixture files, snapshots on disk, dependencies or configuration needed removal. Runtime production behavior is unchanged; the removed exports served only test inventories.

Cross-scope ownership: UI duplicate fleet tests defer to API agent-rows plus UI stale-frame/store integration. UI theme forwarding tests defer to the literal API palette mapping below. Unique UI reasoning-selector/selected-option cases and host messageKind omission tests were explicitly identified as unique and retained by their owners. Daemon workflow wrapper-budget probes defer to the API budget matrix plus real workflow handoff integration.

## Removed seams and retained consumers

The repository-wide non-test reference search found no consumer of `isToolsetId`, `toolsetById`, `toolsetOfTool` or `chatToolNames`; their callers were the deleted API inventory and the daemon MCP registry inventory removed by its owner. `TOOLSETS`, its public types and grant policies remain. `HTML_RENDER_STOCK_PALETTES` and `HTML_RENDER_PALETTE_VARIABLES` keep their internal implementation/type roles but no longer expose values only the deleted UI theme test imported.

Production callers justify retaining the other supporting seams: `appendNodeRequireOption` is used by agent-host env setup, box remote env/sandbox and plugin session env; `emptyKnowledgeLinkingState` by daemon knowledge linking state recovery; handoff format/summary helpers by UI continuation preview and host fork orchestration; permission labels/hints by the composer, launcher and MCP catalog; `UNTRUSTED_NOTICE` by MCP search/message/session/watch responses. Removing their weak direct tests does not make the functions dead. Agent-tools no-op object identity checks also remain because the service uses returned identity to skip unnecessary persisted writes.

## Measured cost and validation

Root baseline: `pnpm test`, Node v24.22.1 on Linux 6.8.0-147-generic x86_64, pnpm 10.12.1; Node test runner + tsx + assert-ok, 4 test files per package, packages sequential, TSX_DISABLE_CACHE=1. API package duration: 19,448.274 ms; config: 7,695.115 ms. These are actual runner/package durations from `/tmp/orquester-test-audit/baseline.log`, not standalone exclusive CPU measurements. No baseline failures appeared in these two package summaries.

Slowest API cases retained: open agent-call aggregate-ceiling retention 466.535 ms, hostile expression parsing 431.190 ms, recipe materialization/validation 294.974 ms, busy-agent open-call retention 258.904 ms, dropped-row retention accounting 255.288 ms and adversarial base64 scanning 249.256 ms. Their size reaches real retention/byte limits or exercises demonstrated hostile-input behavior; reducing data below those boundaries would weaken their contracts. None uses wall-clock sleeping or a real network.

No CI speedup is inferred from test counts; deleting one file removes a worker startup, while other changes reduce repeated setup and maintenance. Final comparable suite timing, repeat variance and repository gates belong to the root report. The initial baseline measurements above are historical observations; root is rerunning the frozen baseline under a recorded external Node-preload checksum before claiming a comparable speedup.

Root executed `python3 /tmp/orquester-test-audit/api-config-regression-probes.py` after the serialized validation slot became available; the driver exited 0. It copied final API/config sources into isolated temporary packages, ran three positive owners (one selected case each), then changed only those copies for three negative controls. All three positive cases passed; each mutant failed at its intended assertion, as confirmed in the logs:

| Probe | Independently observed result |
| --- | --- |
| Palette background channel changed from n-950 to n-100 | Expected literal #112233; mutant returned #aabbcc. |
| Cache serialization omitted resumeGoalAfterRestart | Expected literal true; mutant returned undefined. |
| Preload path quoting removed | Real Node tried the truncated C:\\Program path and exited 1 instead of the required 0. |

The actual selected commands used `TSX_DISABLE_CACHE=1 node --import /var/lib/orquester/workspaces/jaspersito/orquester/node_modules/tsx/dist/loader.mjs --test --test-name-pattern <pattern> <file>`, with argv patterns `palette RGB colors`, `cached head preserves` and `Node loads preloads`; files were respectively `src/agent-chat/html-render.test.ts`, `src/agent-chat/fold-snapshot.test.ts` in the copied API package and `src/tooling.test.ts` in the copied config package. `api_config.regression-probes.json` records all six exact argv descriptions/statuses, and `api-config-probe-*.log` records the assertion output under `/tmp/orquester-test-audit/`. Copies were restored after each mutant and removed afterward; repository production sources were never mutated.

Scoped `git diff --check` passed for the final HTML/MCP-app test and ledger changes. These six probe runs do not claim whole-package coverage: the final changed gate still owns the edited insertion/prepared-script cases, package typechecks and dependent tests. No test process was started by this owner during a held slot.

## How to read the ledger

Each file section supplies its independent contract source, stable public seam, concrete caller-facing failure class, oracle/refactor reasoning, surviving owner and risk. Each named row adds its exact baseline scenario and assertion evidence. Literal independently authored inputs/expected outputs are oracles; parser/serializer identity is retained only where the independently specified lossless persistence boundary is the contract and the fixture is explicit. Counts are data/wire/resource limits, not DOM row/geometry snapshots. Table evidence excerpts are abbreviated for readability; the test body is the precise fixture/assertion source.

The shared six-bar rationale applies only to surviving rows: (1) source is listed per file; (2) the listed failure changes public data/wire/security behavior; (3) authored fixture literals, fixed clock values, independent byte measurement or explicit lossless roundtrip invariant can disagree with the owner; (4) the test calls the exported boundary; (5) implementation/class/layout/source identifiers are unconstrained; (6) each retained row covers the named input/state/protocol condition at its stable owning boundary. Duplicated conditions identified during cross-owner review are removed or consolidated.

## `packages/api/src/agent-chat/agent-rows.test.ts`

Contract: **on-demand cold snapshots and ordered agent-row replacement**. Independent source: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-agent-visuals-and-session-tools-design.md (§1 provenance, §2 visuals, §3 MCP Apps, §6 lineage)`. Production owner: `packages/api/src/agent-chat/agent-rows.ts`. Public snapshot/frame functions; stale request rejection remains in UI store tests. Literal row ids/ownership and exact final window detect dropped, duplicated or misplaced agent work.

Oracle/refactor/uniqueness: assertions below compare observable contract data; internal helpers and implementation structure can change. Larger daemon/UI integration remains for transport, persistence side effects and request ordering, which are distinct from this pure schema/protocol owner. Unknown data is retained/refused according to the listed source, never silently dropped to simplify tests. Supporting exports remain when production consumers use them; the only removed production seams are the four catalog inventory helpers and two test-only palette value exports listed above.

Non-test callers: API modules are consumed through the shared API barrel by daemon agent-host/MCP/workflows and UI state/components; config parsers/path helpers by their matching daemon stores and launchers. File-specific owner details above identify the caller boundary. Risk: high for protocol, auth/privacy, storage and scheduler contracts; lower for removed inventory/prose tests with an identified surviving owner. Validation: package `pnpm test` and `pnpm typecheck`; changed files additionally use the exact focused command in the validation record.

| --- | --- | --- |

Oracle: assert.equal(isOnDemandAgent({ ...base, status: "idle" }), true); assert.equal(isOnDemandAgent({ ...base, status: "failed" }), true); assert.equal(isOnDemandAgent({ ...base, status: "waiting" }), false); assert.equal(isOnDemandAgent({ ...base, status: "pending" }), false); assert.equal(isOnDemandAgent({ agentKind: "background", kind: "subagent", status: "completed" }), false); assert.equal(isOnDemandAgent({ agentKind: "agent", kind: "loop", status: "completed" }), false); assert.equal(isOnDemandAgent({ agentKind: "agent", kind: "goal", status: "completed" }), false) |

## `packages/api/src/agent-chat/fold.retention.test.ts`

Contract: **bounded per-parent/per-agent/aggregate/message retention with open-work exemptions**. Independent source: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-23-fold-performance-design.md (independent window/slack limits and retention exemptions)`. Production owner: `packages/api/src/agent-chat/fold.ts`. Public reducer plus dropped-row output; independent design numbers and literal ids detect lost running calls, compaction boundaries, pending questions, incorrect slack and unbounded windows. These real threshold-sized streams uniquely exercise interacting retention classes.

Oracle/refactor/uniqueness: assertions below compare observable contract data; internal helpers and implementation structure can change. Larger daemon/UI integration remains for transport, persistence side effects and request ordering, which are distinct from this pure schema/protocol owner. Unknown data is retained/refused according to the listed source, never silently dropped to simplify tests. Supporting exports remain when production consumers use them; the only removed production seams are the four catalog inventory helpers and two test-only palette value exports listed above.

Non-test callers: API modules are consumed through the shared API barrel by daemon agent-host/MCP/workflows and UI state/components; config parsers/path helpers by their matching daemon stores and launchers. File-specific owner details above identify the caller boundary. Risk: high for protocol, auth/privacy, storage and scheduler contracts; lower for removed inventory/prose tests with an identified surviving owner. Validation: package `pnpm test` and `pnpm typecheck`; changed files additionally use the exact focused command in the validation record.

| Baseline line and exact test name | Disposition | Specific contract/failure and oracle evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |

Contract: **append-only sequence, streaming, checkpoints, rewind, pending answers and metadata identity**. Public event reducer/snapshot; fixed event order, message ids and statuses detect discarded prompts, replay duplicates, wrong rewind ownership and lost session identity. Retention-specific duplicates move to fold.retention; parser tests cannot replace reducer ordering.

Oracle/refactor/uniqueness: assertions below compare observable contract data; internal helpers and implementation structure can change. Larger daemon/UI integration remains for transport, persistence side effects and request ordering, which are distinct from this pure schema/protocol owner. Unknown data is retained/refused according to the listed source, never silently dropped to simplify tests. Supporting exports remain when production consumers use them; the only removed production seams are the four catalog inventory helpers and two test-only palette value exports listed above.

Non-test callers: API modules are consumed through the shared API barrel by daemon agent-host/MCP/workflows and UI state/components; config parsers/path helpers by their matching daemon stores and launchers. File-specific owner details above identify the caller boundary. Risk: high for protocol, auth/privacy, storage and scheduler contracts; lower for removed inventory/prose tests with an identified surviving owner. Validation: package `pnpm test` and `pnpm typecheck`; changed files additionally use the exact focused command in the validation record.

## `packages/api/src/agent-chat/goal.test.ts`

Contract: **normalized goal/command/capability parsing and state-change detection**. Independent source: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-24-agent-goals-design.md (§4 normalized goals, §5 restart semantics)`. Production owner: `packages/api/src/agent-chat/goal.ts`. Exported parsers/predicates; literal allowed statuses, malformed fields and state deltas detect hiding unfinished work, losing zero/null budgets and honoring unknown actions. Provider adapters consume these normalized contracts.

Oracle/refactor/uniqueness: assertions below compare observable contract data; internal helpers and implementation structure can change. Larger daemon/UI integration remains for transport, persistence side effects and request ordering, which are distinct from this pure schema/protocol owner. Unknown data is retained/refused according to the listed source, never silently dropped to simplify tests. Supporting exports remain when production consumers use them; the only removed production seams are the four catalog inventory helpers and two test-only palette value exports listed above.

Non-test callers: API modules are consumed through the shared API barrel by daemon agent-host/MCP/workflows and UI state/components; config parsers/path helpers by their matching daemon stores and launchers. File-specific owner details above identify the caller boundary. Risk: high for protocol, auth/privacy, storage and scheduler contracts; lower for removed inventory/prose tests with an identified surviving owner. Validation: package `pnpm test` and `pnpm typecheck`; changed files additionally use the exact focused command in the validation record.

| Baseline line and exact test name | Disposition | Specific contract/failure and oracle evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 44: an objective and a status are all a goal needs | KEEP | Protects an objective and a status are all a goal needs. Oracle: assert.deepEqual(parseAgentGoal({ objective: "x", status: "paused" }), { objective: "x", status: "paused" }); assert.deepEqual(parseAgentGoal({ objective: "x", status }), { objective: "x", status }) |
| 54: a goal without a usable objective or status is no goal | KEEP | Protects a goal without a usable objective or status is no goal. Oracle: assert.equal(parseAgentGoal(value), null, label) |
| 71: anything that is not a record is no goal, and nothing makes the parser throw | KEEP | Protects anything that is not a record is no goal, and nothing makes the parser throw. Oracle: assert.equal(parseAgentGoal(value), null, \`expected null for ${String(value)}\`); assert.equal(parseAgentGoal(Object.create(null)), null); assert.equal(parseAgentGoal(new Date()), null) |
| 79: an optional field of the wrong type or range is dropped, and the goal kept | KEEP | Protects an optional field of the wrong type or range is dropped, and the goal kept. Oracle: assert.deepEqual(parseAgentGoal({ ...minimal, ...extra }), minimal, label) |
| 103: a null token budget is kept — the goal has none — and zero is a count, not a gap | KEEP | Protects a null token budget is kept — the goal has none — and zero is a count, not a gap. Oracle: assert.deepEqual(parseAgentGoal({ objective: "x", status: "active", tokenBudget: null }), { objective: "x", status: "active", tokenBudget: null }); assert.deepEqual( parseAgentGoal({ objective: "x", status: "active", rounds: 0, tokensUsed: 0, tokenBudget: 0, elapsedMs: 0 }), { objective: "x", status: "active", rounds: 0, tokensUsed: 0, tokenBudget: 0, elapsedMs: 0 } ) |

Contract: **cross-agent transcript budgeting, anchors, UTF-8 cuts and recoverable omission paging**. Independent source: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-agent-visuals-and-session-tools-design.md (§1 provenance, §2 visuals, §3 MCP Apps, §6 lineage)`. Production owner: `packages/api/src/agent-chat/handoff.ts`. Public handoff selection/render API; fixed history contents, byte constraints and literal paging cursors detect dropped original requests, reordered history, broken Unicode and inaccessible omitted turns. Larger fork tests use this selector and do not own its budget matrix.

Oracle/refactor/uniqueness: assertions below compare observable contract data; internal helpers and implementation structure can change. Larger daemon/UI integration remains for transport, persistence side effects and request ordering, which are distinct from this pure schema/protocol owner. File-specific owner details above identify the caller boundary. Risk: high for protocol, auth/privacy, storage and scheduler contracts; lower for removed inventory/prose tests with an identified surviving owner. Validation: package `pnpm test` and `pnpm typecheck`; changed files additionally use the exact focused command in the validation record.

## `packages/api/src/agent-chat/history-cursor.test.ts`

Contract: **base64url UTF-8 history cursor identity and cross-thread isolation**. Independent source: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-23-thread-index-and-lazy-boot-design.md (cached fold fidelity and history cursors)`. Production owner: `packages/api/src/agent-chat/history-cursor.ts`. Public encoder/decoder; independent JSON keys and literal Unicode ids detect incorrect alphabet, thread confusion, missing sequence bounds and accepting malformed cursors. Index storage must not choose cursor semantics.

## `packages/api/src/agent-chat/message-liveness.test.ts`

## `packages/api/src/agent-chat/roster.test.ts`

Contract: **subagent/background/workflow lifecycle projection and safe stop eligibility**. Independent source: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-21-agent-chat-gui-design.md (§5 reducer, §6 transport, §7 GUI)`. Production owner: `packages/api/src/agent-chat/roster.ts`. Public roster/panel/stop-policy functions; fixed state/attempt/run ids, usage and member results detect reactivating completed retries, double-counting coordinators, hiding orphan members, retaining unsafe session URLs and refusing valid stops. Literal state transitions have independent product meaning.

## `packages/api/src/agent-chat/secret-requests.test.ts`

Contract: **secret values never survive any recorded provider shape**. Production owner: `packages/api/src/agent-chat/secret-requests.ts`. Public redactor/reference/summary parser; independent secret sentinel must disappear while names/outcomes remain, including double serialization and deep inputs. Generic tool projection does not own recursively embedded secret-result forms.

## `packages/api/src/agent-chat/slim.test.ts`

Contract: **bounded transport payloads preserve semantic status, task linkage and goals**. Independent source: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-21-agent-chat-gui-design.md (§5 reducer, §6 transport, §7 GUI)`. Production owner: `packages/api/src/agent-chat/slim.ts`. Public wire projection; independently specified UTF-8 caps, literal task ids and status transitions detect byte inflation, hidden failures or dropped goals/launch prompts. Stored-data bounding has a different byte limit/argument contract; HTML repetitions are consolidated into the real reference consumer.

Oracle/refactor/uniqueness: assertions below compare observable contract data; internal helpers and implementation structure can change. Larger daemon/UI integration remains for transport, persistence side effects and request ordering, which are distinct from this pure schema/protocol owner. Unknown data is retained/refused according to the listed source, never silently dropped to simplify tests. Supporting exports remain when production consumers use them; the only removed production seams are the four catalog inventory helpers and two test-only palette value exports listed above.

Non-test callers: API modules are consumed through the shared API barrel by daemon agent-host/MCP/workflows and UI state/components; config parsers/path helpers by their matching daemon stores and launchers. File-specific owner details above identify the caller boundary. Risk: high for protocol, auth/privacy, storage and scheduler contracts; lower for removed inventory/prose tests with an identified surviving owner. Validation: package `pnpm test` and `pnpm typecheck`; changed files additionally use the exact focused command in the validation record.

## `packages/api/src/agent-chat/thread-head.test.ts`

## `packages/api/src/workflows/graph.test.ts`

Contract: **workflow readiness/skip/edge liveness at branches, joins and errors**. Independent source: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-automated-workflows-design.md (§3 grammar/graphs, §4 blocks, §6 schedules, §7 validation, §8 atomic patches)`. Production owner: `packages/api/src/workflows/graph.ts`. Public graph/readiness API; manually enumerated ready/skip nodes for each graph detect early joins, running untaken branches, lost error handlers and duplicate execution after first-merge. Engine integration adds side effects; these cases own the scheduling decision matrix.

Oracle/refactor/uniqueness: assertions below compare observable contract data; internal helpers and implementation structure can change. Larger daemon/UI integration remains for transport, persistence side effects and request ordering, which are distinct from this pure schema/protocol owner. Unknown data is retained/refused according to the listed source, never silently dropped to simplify tests. Supporting exports remain when production consumers use them; the only removed production seams are the four catalog inventory helpers and two test-only palette value exports listed above.

Non-test callers: API modules are consumed through the shared API barrel by daemon agent-host/MCP/workflows and UI state/components; config parsers/path helpers by their matching daemon stores and launchers. File-specific owner details above identify the caller boundary. Risk: high for protocol, auth/privacy, storage and scheduler contracts; lower for removed inventory/prose tests with an identified surviving owner. Validation: package `pnpm test` and `pnpm typecheck`; changed files additionally use the exact focused command in the validation record.

## `packages/api/src/workflows/templates.test.ts`

Contract: **starter workflows remain disabled/valid and emitted Jira programs actually work**. Independent source: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-automated-workflows-design.md (§3 grammar/graphs, §4 blocks, §6 schedules, §7 validation, §8 atomic patches)`. Production owner: `packages/api/src/workflows/templates.ts`. Public template/create/validate API and dynamically imported emitted JavaScript; fixed project/timezone/Jira records and literal transition HTTP calls detect unintended auto-run, stale model defaults, bad Jira output mapping and moving the wrong ticket. Mocks isolate Jira transport while executing the real template source.

Oracle/refactor/uniqueness: assertions below compare observable contract data; internal helpers and implementation structure can change. Larger daemon/UI integration remains for transport, persistence side effects and request ordering, which are distinct from this pure schema/protocol owner. Unknown data is retained/refused according to the listed source, never silently dropped to simplify tests. Supporting exports remain when production consumers use them; the only removed production seams are the four catalog inventory helpers and two test-only palette value exports listed above.

Non-test callers: API modules are consumed through the shared API barrel by daemon agent-host/MCP/workflows and UI state/components; config parsers/path helpers by their matching daemon stores and launchers. File-specific owner details above identify the caller boundary. Risk: high for protocol, auth/privacy, storage and scheduler contracts; lower for removed inventory/prose tests with an identified surviving owner. Validation: package `pnpm test` and `pnpm typecheck`; changed files additionally use the exact focused command in the validation record.
